# LOCO Agent — Project Progress Tracker

**Last reconciled:** 2026-11-26  
**Canonical tracker:** This file is the source of truth for roadmap status, task ordering, acceptance criteria, dependencies, and estimates. Update it in the same PR whenever scope or task status changes.

## How to use this tracker

1. Before starting work, check this file and the linked PR/issue state.
2. Work only on the next unblocked task in the ordered plan unless a security dependency requires otherwise.
3. Every task must have an acceptance condition and evidence (tests, exact-head CI, review, or release verification).
4. Update the status, evidence, dependency, and estimate when work changes. Do not mark a task complete because code was submitted or a PR is mergeable.
5. A PR being CI-green is not equivalent to security acceptance or merge. Stacked PRs must be merged in dependency order after review.
6. Keep this tracker current in the same PR that changes the plan. Add a dated entry to the change log below.
7. Do not close a PR until its change is integrated or intentionally superseded and its dependent branches have been reconciled.

## Status definitions

- **Not started:** no implementation/design work yet.
- **Design:** requirements or architecture being specified; no accepted implementation.
- **In progress:** implementation or tests underway.
- **Submitted:** implementation exists in an open PR; validation/review incomplete.
- **CI green:** exact PR head passed its required CI, but review/integration may remain.
- **Partially complete:** meaningful evidence exists, but one or more exit criteria remain.
- **Blocked:** cannot safely proceed until a named dependency is resolved.
- **Complete:** all acceptance criteria pass, required independent review is recorded, integrated into the intended branch, and release/security limitations are documented.

### Mainline reality and current product baseline

The live `main` tree still contains the original TypeScript VS Code extension, compiled `out/` artifacts/source maps, npm manifest/lockfile, and older product/security documentation. It does **not** contain `core/loco-core`, Rust authority runtime, current canonical tracker, or the newer artifact/provenance workflows. `src/extension.ts` on main still directly creates/uses the TypeScript `LOCOAgent`; it does not perform the intended Rust authority startup/registration path.

The protocol v2/v2.1 stack is present on separate branches. The inspected authority binary on `impl/authority-approval-protocol-v21` still contains the v1 protocol path and branches to v2/v2.1 handling only when the incoming protocol version says 2.0 or 2.1. These are component branches; production activation and normal user work have not been cut over.

Main `README.md` still describes “auto-apply patches” and claims “Ready for production deployment”. These statements are inconsistent with the documented disabled/controlled security status and should be corrected before release. Main `package.json` retains TypeScript-ESLint v6, `@vscode/vsce@^3.7.1`, and `uuid@^9`; the checked-in lockfile root metadata has stale/extra root entries (including axios and jest-util) compared with the manifest. The historical npm audit counts in old PRs are baselines only; the current production manifest+lockfile must be freshly audited immediately before dependency promotion and release.

### Confirmed current findings

1. **Governance and tracker are not integrated.** #148 remains draft on a tracker branch; main is not protected by enforced required checks/review in the observed branch API response. The tracker must be accepted and merged early after its scope/order is agreed; protection must be set before production integration.
2. **Dependency remediation remains candidate-only.** PRs #155–#166 hold diagnostic and remediation workflows. No production dependency manifest/lockfile promotion has been accepted. TypeScript-ESLint v7 (#161) and v8 (#162) are alternatives, not cumulative updates. The v8 branch's exact head `198645acb8639362c28032387044ff9ace5b930b` passed Node 20/22 TypeScript CI and related lockfile workflows; its candidate audit artifact still contained 23 records (17 high, 5 moderate, 1 low), so it is not accepted. The semver-safe candidate #163 exact run #45797728997 succeeded and produced 5 records (2 high Azure and 3 moderate tooling), while #165's exact run #45794905621 passed its Node 20/22 matrix and left two high Azure records with its candidate overrides. Both are candidate artifacts, not promoted production dependency state.
3. **PR #166 now has exact-head green candidate CI, but audit is not clean.** Corrected workflow commit `6025b8ffed313db2828161687c5359a38bf2b814` passed exact-head run [#45801230999](https://github.com/Veeresh-11/Loco-Agent/actions/runs/45801230999). Node 20.20.2 and Node 22 clean installs, compile, Jest (38 tests), lint and VSIX packaging all passed. Candidate lock graph places `@azure/identity@4.13.0` and `@azure/msal-node@3.8.10` beneath `@vscode/vsce`, with Identity engine `>=20.0.0` and MSAL engine `>=16`. Fresh audit artifact #15108115415, SHA-256 `870c0e123bce8476f8d2e60820d8ff8dbc8b43f85936a9acd91109a673b92a76`, reports **3 moderate records, 0 high, 0 critical**: Azure Identity (via MSAL Node), MSAL Node (via uuid), and uuid (`uuid@8.3.2`, advisory range <11.1.1). This does not clear all advisories. #166 remains draft/open; no independent review submitted; production manifests remain unchanged.
4. **Azure 4.13.0 is a plausible candidate, not a release acceptance.** The published Azure Identity 4.13.0 manifest uses MSAL Node ^3.5.0 and Node >=20. The candidate run proves lock metadata and project checks for LOCO's current workflow, but still needs API/call-site compatibility tests, fresh complete audit after uuid remediation, and independent dependency review. Do not promote overrides simply because their CI passes.
5. **Native filesystem security is partially implemented, not certified.** Linux/Windows platform runs and native probes are recorded in the workstream history; supported kernels, filesystems, OS versions, FFI ABI, mount/reparse behavior, races, post-open verification and failure semantics still need a bounded explicit support matrix and independent review. macOS firmlink issue #74 remains open; do not treat `SF_NOUNLINK`/generic `sunlnk` as `SF_FIRMLINK`. If a deterministic native firmlink proof cannot be established, affected configurations must remain explicitly unsupported/fail-closed.
6. **Approval binding is not authenticated human presence.** #149–#151 provide design, challenge binding and v2.1 components. Issue #82 remains open for platform-backed authenticator assertions, user presence/user verification, app/RP identity, nonce and action digest verification, enrollment/recovery/revocation, remote/headless behavior, and independent review. A challenge binding check alone is not authenticated approval.
7. **Production authority integration is still missing.** #128–#138 and #149–#151 provide native registration, DTO, session, client, handler and challenge components on separate stacks. #139 records the integration gap: activation, task execution, reads/disclosure, writes, patch actions and commands still are not routed through Rust authority on main. Process-local registry handles are identifiers, not OS capabilities or authentication. Integration must register/capture/revoke natively and fail closed if authority startup, protocol negotiation, IPC or verification fails.
8. **Controlled writes and command execution are not production-ready.** Existing design/prototype PRs #22–#25 and surrounding roadmap work do not prove full implementation. No write/command capability is enabled until native filesystem acceptance, Rust authority production cutover, authenticated approval, bounded policy, rollback/recovery, output/time/cancel controls and adversarial integration tests have passed.
9. **Artifact build is not artifact trust.** #140–#147 and #152–#154 provide platform build, action pinning, provenance/verification design and package-boundary work in draft branches. Build success alone does not verify trusted-main provenance. Need a verifier that validates provenance/attestation against trusted policy and exact executable digest, signing and protected installation by platform, no unsafe PATH/workspace/temp/env fallback, race-resistant launch binding, SBOM/license checks, and independent review.
10. **Legacy product branch chain is not mainline.** #6–#8 represent a validated-feature/retrieval/path-test chain, but remain open, old and separate from current main. Review provider streaming/fallback, RAG retrieval/evaluation/deduplication, patch preview/rollback and Extension Host behavior; transfer only the changes that pass current requirements and tests.
11. **No release-ready claim is supported.** Current main's README production statement and auto-apply wording must be corrected. End-to-end tests, packaging integrity, signing, trust verification, upgrade/uninstall/rollback and final reviewer sign-off remain required.

**Effort estimate:** 33–63 working days of primary implementation/integration/release work plus the secondary audit included above, assuming one primary engineer, focused scope, available test runners and no major redesign. The estimate excludes unpredictable external review turnaround, platform hardware/host limitations, signing account setup, marketplace approval and remediation discoveries. Calendar time can extend to roughly 8–14 weeks with one engineer and external waits. This replaces the older 43–88 day / 9–16 week broad estimate because the current plan explicitly avoids major new-version features and groups duplicate candidate/stack work rather than treating every PR as additive.

### Merge and branch-consolidation strategy

The target is one authoritative `main` containing all accepted existing-TODO work, with every retained branch either merged or explicitly marked as a deliberate temporary exception. “Main ahead of all existing branches” is a final cleanup condition, not a reason to force-merge unrelated histories.

1. Protect `main` before implementation merges: required CI, at least one independent approving review for security-sensitive changes, stale-approval dismissal on new commits, no direct pushes, restricted bypass, and signed/reproducible release gate as appropriate.
2. Merge in dependency order, not PR-number order. The dependency chain for current package candidates is #155 -> #156 -> #157/#158 -> #159 -> choose #161 **or** #162 -> #163 -> #164 -> #165 -> #166, with the final winner determined by fresh combined audit, runtime/package compatibility, review and lockfile correctness. Some candidates may be superseded rather than merged. Do not merge both ESLint v7 and v8 alternatives.
3. Complete filesystem support gates before enabling any privileged workspace write. Complete authority registration, snapshot path, protocol and IPC before approving runtime operations. Complete authenticated approval before writes or commands.
4. Integrate Rust protocol v2/v2.1 as an explicit supported version with downgrade/fallback policy. Do not assume separate DTO/client/handler branches are one coherent stack until their merge bases and exact tree are reconciled.
5. After each merge, run CI on the resulting main SHA, inspect the merge diff, rerun relevant platform/security tests and update this tracker. Do not bulk-merge draft PRs.
6. The final main branch must contain every accepted required commit. For each remaining branch, compare its full ancestry and diff with final main. A branch can be closed/deleted only if its unique changes were integrated/transferred or explicitly rejected and the decision is recorded. If a retained branch has unique needed work, main is not yet final.
7. Do not use force pushes to manufacture an “ahead of all branches” appearance. Merge or cherry-pick accepted unique commits, reconcile branch bases, and close branches only after secondary review.

### Mandatory security, code-review and release checklist

A feature is not done until all applicable gates are evidenced:

- Static analysis, type checking, test suite, lint, dependency audit and secret scan on the exact final commit.
- SAST/dependency/SBOM/license review, pinned GitHub Actions, least-privilege workflow permissions and provenance verification.
- Rust unsafe/FFI review, ABI assertions, memory/size bounds, error mapping and malformed input testing.
- IPC framing, schema/version negotiation, peer identity, replay, request caps, timeouts, cancellation, child process lifetime and teardown tests.
- Filesystem path traversal, symlink/reparse/firmlink, mount crossing, hard-link/special-file, TOCTOU, rename/replacement races, file identity, partial reads, and unsupported-filesystem fail-closed behavior.
- Approval challenge replay/expiry/rollback, OS user presence, binding to exact action+snapshot, revocation and cross-process persistence.
- Writes/patches: strict input grammar, handle-relative operation, no unintended overwrite, rollback/recovery and audit trail.
- Commands: fixed executable policy, arguments without shell interpolation, bounded environment/cwd, output/time limits, cancellation and approval.
- Platform package contents, executable digest launch binding, signing/notarization or protected install, upgrade/uninstall/rollback and VS Code Extension Host end-to-end validation.
- Independent reviewer (not the author) signs off sensitive code and final mainline. Record accepted residual risks and exact supported platform versions. Do not state “secure” or “production-ready” as an absolute guarantee; describe residual constraints.

### Secondary audit — scheduled by phase, not background job

The secondary audit is a required final project phase (order 10), to start only after orders 0–9 are complete and release candidate is frozen. This is not a scheduled recurring automation. Use an independent reviewer who did not author the implementation. The reviewer must inspect final main and all remaining PRs/branches, compare ancestry/diffs and unique commits, inspect CI/reviews/thread resolution, workflows/permissions, package and lockfiles, secrets, native FFI/filesystem/IPC, approval/replay, launch verification, privilege boundaries and docs. Record a disposition for every remaining PR/branch:
- **Merge** — unique required changes validated and integrated.
- **Transfer/cherry-pick** — useful commits moved to the final integration branch with destination SHA.
- **Close as superseded** — complete change is contained in another accepted PR/main.
- **Close as not planned** — outside agreed release scope, with rationale.
- **Retain temporarily** — named dependency/review is still outstanding, with owner/exit criteria.

Only after that classification may unwanted PRs be closed and branches deleted. Final audit then re-runs all gates on final main and records main SHA, complete PR/branch inventory, every closure/deletion reason, independent reviewer sign-off and documented residual risks.

### Release-scope guard

Keep in this release: fixes to existing requirements, dependency/vulnerability remediation, compatibility, platform correctness, security, tests, CI, packaging, documentation and integration necessary to make existing TODOs actually work. Put new providers, general autonomous coding, new UI/product workflows, remote authority mode, broad new filesystem support, multi-workspace concurrency expansion and other substantial capabilities into a next-version backlog. A newly discovered small hardening change is in scope when it closes a security or correctness gap in an existing task; large capability growth is not.

### Audit limitations

This repository audit reconciles the full branch and PR counts and inspects important live source/evidence, but it is not the final independent line-by-line review of all 145 open PRs. The connector could not retrieve every branch tip/PR diff/review/CI artifact within the single-pass call budget, and the branch-protection endpoint was denied on the latest attempted read. The final secondary audit is the explicit mechanism to close these gaps. No PRs were merged or closed, and no branches were deleted in this reconciliation.

| D3 | Python AI service extraction | **Pending** | Minimal versioned service contract for existing provider/RAG/evaluation responsibilities; bounded lifecycle/backpressure/cancellation; package and test; no model-output trust. |
| D4 | TypeScript production integration | **Component migrations only; live path remains outside Rust** | Start verified authority only after runtime trust gate; actual registration/capture; migrate workspace/process/disclosure paths and retire direct privileged bypasses. |
| D5 | Full product end-to-end | **Not accepted/demonstrated** | Complete VS Code workflow; provider fallback/streaming/retrieval/evaluation and safe modification; privacy/permission/adversarial tests; Extension Host smoke tests. |
| D6 | Packaging/release | **Not release-ready** | Trusted provenance/signing, SBOM/license/audit, exact package boundary, install/upgrade/uninstall/rollback, release docs and independent sign-off. |

**Legacy feature stack:** #6–#8 remain open non-draft PRs; #1–#5 were closed unmerged. Evaluate them separately for streaming/provider behavior, retrieval baseline/dedup, patch preview/rollback and Extension Host validation. Do not merge stale ancestry without reviewing diffs and exact current checks.

**Branch merge and cleanup policy**

- Merge accepted PRs into one integration chain in dependency order using the exact current head SHA; never bulk-merge the stack or merge children before parents.
- After each merge, run CI on the resulting mainline commit, rerun affected platform tests, inspect the merge diff and update this tracker.
- Close a PR only after its unique commits are merged/transferred or explicitly rejected and dependent branches are reconciled.
- Compare remaining branch tips to final main only in the cleanup phase. Delete only branches with no required unique commit and no active dependency; branches with unique work must be merged/transferred or explicitly deferred.
- Run the mandatory secondary audit after task completion and freeze, not concurrently with feature integration. Keep a disposition record for every PR closure and branch deletion.

**Mandatory post-completion secondary audit:** Start after orders 0–9 are complete and the release candidate is frozen. A reviewer who did not author the implementation must audit final main and every remaining open PR/branch for ancestry/unique commits, CI/review threads, docs, secrets, manifests/lockfiles, workflow permissions, artifact contents, IPC, filesystem/path/handle boundaries, approval replay, launch verification and TypeScript bypasses. Classify each as merge, transfer/cherry-pick, close-as-superseded, close-as-not-planned or retain. Record reason and destination commit for every closure/deletion. Only then close PRs and delete branches proven unwanted. Finish with complete tests/scans on final main and reviewer sign-off.

**Scope guard:** Include small security fixes, bugs, compatibility, regression tests, CI, docs and integration needed for current acceptance criteria. Defer new providers, generalized autonomy, UI redesign, arbitrary command capabilities, broad filesystem support, multi-workspace concurrency, remote authority and new product workflows to the next version.

## F. Delivery estimate

**Estimate basis:** one primary engineer, with independent security review and platform test support available as needed. Working days represent engineering effort and exclude idle waiting for reviewers, signing credentials, administrator policy changes or runner availability. Tasks overlap where safe; do not mechanically sum every row.

| Milestone | Remaining effort | Dependencies / confidence |
|---|---:|---|
| E0 #165 workflow repair + full exact-head candidate CI | 1–2 days | High; current failure is a candidate-workflow JSON newline defect. |
| E1 dependency decisions, artifact review, independent review and production lock/manifest update | 2–4 days | Medium; API compatibility and Node 20 policy may change scope. |
| E2 main governance, tracker/README correction, clean main CI | 1–3 days | Medium; requires admin config and independent PR review. |
| E3 critical current-head CI/review refresh and branch ancestry mapping | 2–4 days | Medium-low; large stack and runner/connector availability. |
| E4 native filesystem acceptance and independent platform review | 3–8 days | Low; issue #74 may require limiting supported macOS scope. |
| E5 integrated registry/protocol/snapshot read-only path | 3–6 days | Medium-low; depends on E3/E4 and stack reconciliation. |
| E6 platform authenticator and independent review | 5–12 days | Low; API differences and Linux availability are external. |
| E7 trusted provenance verifier, signing and secure sidecar launch | 5–10 days | Low; signing identity, trust root and exact-file launch design. |
| E8 production TS/Rust migration and bypass retirement | 4–8 days | Medium; needs accepted E5–E7. |
| E9 controlled writes and command authorization | 7–13 days | Low; needs all prior security gates and adversarial testing. |
| E10 Python service extraction and existing product integration | 5–9 days | Medium; design can overlap, final integration cannot. |
| E11 final end-to-end, release and secondary audit/branch cleanup | 5–9 days | Medium-low; depends on prior work and reviewer availability. |

**Planning range:** approximately **43–88 working days** of remaining effort, with overlap. For one primary developer and sequential security gates, plan around **9–16 calendar weeks** after reviewer/admin/signing prerequisites are available. External wait time can extend beyond that. The earlier **6–10-day** estimate is only a narrow A1–A7 security-core checkpoint; it is not full product completion or release.

**Small updates vs next version:** include only changes required to make existing acceptance criteria safe, testable, compatible, supportable and releasable. Major features outside the current roadmap should be deferred to a separate next-version backlog.

**Estimate confidence:** medium-low overall; revisit after E0–E4, when dependency and native-support uncertainties are better resolved.

- **2026-11-25 (PR #143 pinned-action manifest audit):** Reviewed the PR #143 workflow diff and fetched the `action.yml` manifest at each of its six exact pinned action commits. Confirmed checkout's default `persist-credentials: true` stores the default GitHub token in local Git config; PR #145 now explicitly disables this for all three checkout uses. The Rust toolchain action defaults to moving `stable` and bootstraps rustup by downloading an installer over HTTPS and piping it to the shell; immutable action SHA therefore does not make the selected compiler reproducible. Upload/download-artifact and setup-node are Node 20 JavaScript actions. The pinned build-provenance action's composite manifest calls two further actions at immutable SHAs (predicate `1176ef556905f349f669722abf30bce1a6e16e01`, attest `ce27ba3b4a9a139d9a20a4a07d69fabb52f1e5bc`); the predicate manifest uses Node 20, while the attest action package declares semver-ranged runtime dependencies that are bundled into its distribution. This is a limited manifest-level audit, not a complete source/bundle/dependency or tag-to-SHA security review. No confirmed exploitable defect was established. Follow-up: independent reviewer to verify source and bundled dependency provenance; decide whether to pin an explicit Rust toolchain version for reproducible builds. PR #143 remains draft.

The `v4` tags for `actions/checkout`, `actions/upload-artifact`, `actions/download-artifact` and `actions/setup-node` each resolve directly to the exact commit SHA pinned in PR #143. The annotated `actions/attest-build-provenance` `v2` tag dereferences to the exact pinned commit `e8998f949152b193b063cb0ec769d69d929409be`; GitHub reports the annotated tag object as unsigned (`verification.verified=false`, reason `unsigned`), so this is a tag target match, not a cryptographic tag-signature validation. `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` is pinned to the current `stable` branch head `39008827e6b4319a349e9a42dfc90579dd25f1ca`, not a version tag, reinforcing the moving-toolchain/reproducibility limitation. - **2026-11-25 (VSIX packaging hygiene audit; follow-up queued):** Inspected the exact-head PR #153 package job #45595708957 logs. `vsce package` emitted warnings because neither `.vscodeignore` nor a `package.json` `files` allowlist exists; it reported 284 packaged files (4.69 MB), including `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` (32.75 KB) and 76 files under `node_modules/` (120.4 KB). No exclusions were applied in this audit to avoid breaking runtime resolution. D6 now tracks a bounded packaging-hygiene follow-up: derive an explicit allowlist from runtime imports and workflow staging, exclude only proven build/test/development material, and require VSIX manifest/content assertions plus all four native executable archive checks. This is a packaging-quality finding, not a release-readiness or security-acceptance claim.

  Separately inspected Microsoft's MSAL Node 3.8.9 source at tag `msal-node-v3.8.10` (the source package manifest in that tag reports 3.8.9): `GuidGenerator.generateGuid()` imports and calls only the named `uuid.v4` API; `CryptoProvider.createNewGuid()` delegates to that generator, and `CryptoProvider` is exported from the package. The UUID advisory GHSA-w5hq-g745-h8pq is specifically an unchecked output-buffer bounds issue in v3/v5/v6, with fix 11.1.1; it does not describe the v4 API exercised by MSAL. This narrows the compatibility question but does not change the dependency semver contract: MSAL 3.8.10 declares `uuid ^8.3.0`, while candidate #168 resolves 11.1.1 outside that range. Strengthened PR [#168](https://github.com/Veeresh-11/Loco-Agent/pull/168) with candidate-only tests on Node 20 and 22 that exercise the actual exported `CryptoProvider.createNewGuid()` path against UUID 11.1.1, in addition to the existing module/API smoke test. ## Dependency candidate: current decision

### PR #171 is the leading candidate, not production-approved

- PR #171: [isolate VSCE 4.1 packaging toolchain on Node 22](https://github.com/Veeresh-11/Loco-Agent/pull/171); open/draft; head `6c10d33d8967428eb1882708d4041e5ee16d824f`; base `security/azure-node20-compatible-candidate`.
- Its candidate workflow [#45809718093](https://github.com/Veeresh-11/Loco-Agent/actions/runs/45809718093) succeeded. Artifact #15111510993 was uploaded and PR description reports zero findings for both separated dependency graphs. PR description also reports standard TypeScript CI #45810075549 and standard npm audit #45810079190 succeeded and that the standard audit artifact reported zero findings.
- PR #171's own validation description explicitly records risks: root `glob` 11/13 and `minimatch` 9/10 are overridden to newer major lines; VSCE's declared `minimatch ^10.2.6` is overridden to 11.0.9; packaging requires Node 22. CI passing does not establish supported upstream API compatibility.
- Review list checked in this execution returned no submitted review for #171. Do not mark accepted, ready, or production-clean until an independent dependency/API reviewer accepts the exact lockfiles and overrides, Node 22 packaging policy is agreed, and full application-level Azure auth and packaging smoke tests pass.
- #170 is not the best final candidate: its own description states three high Azure/MSAL findings remained. #168/#169 are alternate Azure/MSAL explorations. #161 (TS-ESLint v7) and #162 (v8) are alternatives, not both to merge; #162's previously inspected audit still had 23 records. #163–#166 are diagnostic candidate PRs, not production dependency changes.
- No production npm manifest/lockfile promotion is accepted by this update.

### Active dependency decision task

1. Obtain a reviewer who did not author the candidate changes.
2. Review every root and tool lockfile entry and every `overrides` rule; map the actual consuming API call sites for `glob`, `minimatch`, and `brace-expansion`; add direct regression tests for those call sites.
3. Decide whether the repository will formally require Node 22 for packaging while retaining Node 20 for extension application checks. Update contributor setup, CI and release docs accordingly.
4. Test actual authentication-provider flows that use Azure/MSAL, including token acquisition success/failure, refresh, tenant/client configuration, error mapping and cancellation. Package and install the resulting VSIX in a clean VS Code test host.
5. If review accepts the proposal, move only the final exact dependency graph into the production manifest/lockfiles in a dedicated promotion PR. Run a fresh audit from the committed lockfiles on exact main candidate head; inspect the full JSON, not merely summary counts.
6. If the review rejects cross-major overrides, test a semver-compatible or isolated toolchain alternative and preserve the affected dependency rather than silently bypassing package contracts.

| 6 | Command execution authority | #20–#24 plus #94–#99 and integration tasks | Rust-enforced executable identity, executable allowlist, argument/env/cwd policy, no unsafe shell interpolation, process tree isolation, deadline/output/cancel bounds, approval and audit; prove no TS bypass | 3–6 days |
| 7 | Python service and bridges — minimum existing scope | Roadmap priorities 6–7 | Inventory existing Python model/RAG functions; define bounded schema and lifecycle; transport/error/timeout/cancellation/backpressure; connect to current TS/Rust route; tests. | 9 | Release readiness and primary code/security review | #140–#154 and release docs | Reproducible builds; verifier for trusted provenance and exact file digest; signing/notarization or documented protected install; SBOM/license/dependency audits; VSIX archive checks; install/upgrade/rollback; independent code review and release sign-off | 3–6 days |
| 10 | Secondary audit and branch/PR cleanup | All open PRs, branches, issues | Independent reviewer audits final main and every open PR/branch. Classify each unique commit; integrate needed code, transfer, supersede or reject with reasons. Close PRs and delete branches only after proving all useful changes are included in main or explicitly deferred/rejected. Verify main is at least as advanced as every retained/reviewed branch; no required work stranded | 3–6 days |

**Planning estimate:** 33–63 focused working days for the remaining existing scope, including the final secondary review/cleanup allowance. This assumes one primary engineer, existing CI runners and no major redesign. Add independent reviewer turnaround, platform hardware/API access, signing credentials and marketplace approval as external elapsed time; calendar completion can be roughly 8–14 weeks with a single engineer, and can extend if review or native-platform proof is delayed. Treat estimates as ranges, not delivery promises.

## Merge strategy — target one authoritative main

"Main ahead of all existing branches" means every required accepted commit is represented in main and every surviving branch has been classified. It does **not** mean force-merging all 169+ branch tips or merging every PR.

1. Protect main first: require exact CI checks, independent reviewer approval, stale review dismissal after new commits, restrict direct push and bypass, and require status checks for any merge.
2. Rebase/recreate PR #148's documentation-only changes onto current main; do not merge its 183-commit branch history blindly.
3. Choose one compatible production dependency path. #171 is the current leading candidate but must be independently reviewed and tested; do not merge all alternative candidate PRs.
4. Merge native filesystem chain in tested dependency order only after independent acceptance. Keep issue #74 open or narrow platform support if authoritative firmlink proof remains unavailable. Keep issue #82 open until authenticated approval is implemented/reviewed.
5. Merge authority components in a reconciled order: native registry/factory -> protocol DTO/session/handlers -> TypeScript client -> snapshot capture -> approval issue/consume; ensure exact tree and integration tests at each merge.
6. Only implement/enable writes after native filesystem acceptance + Rust authority + authenticated approval. Only implement/enable commands after separate command policy and sandbox/identity/audit gates.
7. Merge artifact build/provenance and verifier only after trusted policy, exact-file binding, signing/protected installation and adversarial replacement tests.
8. After each merge, run tests on the new main SHA, inspect resulting diffs, update tracker and ensure no dependent PR targets stale or unrelated bases.
9. At the end, perform the independent secondary audit against main. For each open PR and branch, record: head SHA, base SHA, unique commits, changed files, CI applicability, reviews/threads, merge ancestry, destination commit, action (merge/transfer/close/defer/reject). Only then close superseded/duplicate/rejected PRs and delete branches.
10. Finally fetch every branch tip and compare it to final main. Any branch with required unique work means consolidation is not finished. Do not force-push branches to create a cosmetic ahead count.

## Mandatory quality, security and threat-review gates

### Per-task / per-PR review
- Exact-head CI: Rust tests, TypeScript compile/Jest/lint, contract/schema checks, native C/SDK probes, package boundary and target OS tests as applicable.
- Static analysis, format, type checks, dependency audit and secret scanning; pin actions by full commit SHA; least-privilege workflow permissions; no persisted checkout credentials for untrusted build steps.
- Inspect changes for input validation, path traversal, symlink/reparse/mount races, TOCTOU, identity/content mismatch, bounds/overflow, IPC spoofing, replay, expiry, privilege escalation, logging of secrets, error disclosure, cancellation and cleanup.
- Review unsafe Rust/FFI, ABI layouts, native API contracts, platform/version differences and fail-closed behavior.
- Unit, integration, negative, adversarial and regression tests. For security-critical features require independent review before merge/enablement.

### Final primary and secondary audit
- Independent reviewer must not be an implementation author for the changes being audited.
- Full diff from main, every unique commit, branch ancestry and PR discussions/review threads.
- SAST and dependency vulnerability scan; SBOM and license policy; secret scan; provenance/attestation and binary digest verification.
- Native filesystem and process-boundary threat model; IPC framing/size/authentication, local attacker/same-user malware, workspace hostile-content, package tampering, update/revocation and rollback.
- Review all public README/docs claims against actual runtime capability. Remove any unearned "production-ready", auto-apply, trusted, secure or supported-platform claims.
- Verify CI checks are required at the repository ruleset level, not merely passing opportunistically.

### Security capabilities that remain disabled until accepted
- Workspace writes and patch application.
- Arbitrary command execution.
- Sidecar launch as an enforcement authority in the production user path.
- Human approval grants / execution tokens without a supported OS-backed authenticator and Rust-side verification.
- Broad macOS firmlink support outside the exact tested/accepted scope.

## Small updates vs next-version scope

**Allowed in this delivery:** bug fixes, security hardening, test coverage, type safety, bounded input/output, accessibility/usability corrections, docs, CI reliability, supported-platform compatibility and small changes needed to complete stated acceptance criteria.

**Defer to next version:** new provider families, major conversational UX redesign, generalized autonomous file editing, remote execution, new marketplace integrations, new broad RAG workflows and features that expand permissions or threat surface without being in the current TODO. Put deferred items in a separate "Next version" backlog with no impact on this release's completion estimate.

This is evidence only for those hosted builds and exercised APFS System/Data volume-group configuration; it is not a universal macOS/APFS guarantee. Non-APFS roots are rejected by the current adapter admission check; earlier/future releases and unusual APFS layouts remain unverified.
- Reviewed the firmlink boundary design. The adapter uses getattrlistbulk + ATTR_CMNEXT_NOFIRMLINKPATH as an early, deny-only preflight, then checks the opened intermediate/final descriptor's f_fsid against the retained workspace root before reading content. Tests demonstrate System/Data f_fsid separation even where st_dev is equal, and show O_RESOLVE_BENEATH does not itself reject the tested firmlink transitions. The original SF_FIRMLINK versus observed SF_NOUNLINK discrepancy remains unexplained by public XNU sources; no user-created symlink/APFS image is a valid substitute for an OS firmlink fixture. The handle-identity path is a useful architectural fallback but still requires independent race/threat review and authoritative acceptance of the precise supported model.
- Reviewed Windows evidence. The x64 SDK ABI probe and Server 2022 build 20348 / Server 2025 build 26100 NTFS tests passed, including junction/reparse and secondary-volume fixtures. Microsoft documentation still does not expressly reconcile the exact user-mode NtCreateFile + RootDirectory + OBJ_DONT_REPARSE combination; moderator answers conflict in strength and are not a version-stable API contract. Retain post-open reparse/file-identity checks and fail closed; do not claim universal Windows support.
- PR #112 has no submitted independent review or inline review threads. It remains draft; no code was merged and no security capability was enabled.
- **Order 2 result: Partially complete / awaiting independent native security review and API-contract resolution.** Exact-head cross-platform CI and contract validation are green. Remaining gates: authoritative Apple reconciliation or formally accepted bounded handle-identity architecture; documented Windows support contract or narrower supported policy; adversarial review of races and native FFI; exact support matrix acceptance; and stack reconciliation onto a clean main-based integration branch before merge.

## Code review register — started 2026-11-26

### Scope and counting method

This register covers the currently identified implementation/candidate PR stack and packaging candidate: #112, #128, #130, #131, #133, #135, #136, #137, #138, #140, #141, #143, #145, #147, #150, #151, #152, #153, #154, #155, and #171. Documentation-only PRs #129, #132, #134, #139, #142, and #146 are design/evidence inputs, not counted as code-review units. Superseded failed workflow PR #144 is not counted separately; its descendant #145 is in scope. This is the currently identified candidate set, not yet a repository-wide proof that no other active code PRs exist.

A review unit is one code-bearing PR, including changed source, tests, fixtures, workflow, or packaging files. “Reviewed” means a first-pass source review was actually performed here; it does not mean independent approval. GitHub submitted review state is recorded separately. PRs may overlap files because they are stacked; percentage is PR coverage, not unique-file, line, or risk-weighted coverage.

## Execution update — 2026-11-26: Order 3 PR #131 native workspace adapter factory first-pass review

- Re-read the canonical tracker on `docs/tracker-mainline-reconciliation` and refreshed PR #131 metadata, exact head, complete changed-file list, patch, review submissions, inline threads and head-associated workflows.
- **PR #131 status:** open, draft, mergeable; base `security/rust-workspace-registry-v1` at `4142b0dd89fab1dff745865104d4f4aefe9c9ee1`; head `security/native-workspace-adapter-factory-v1` at `dc4bb925f8a0c86c3fb30c502144debc333bfc90`. Compare reports 10 commits ahead / 0 behind; the factory branch is directly based on the reviewed registry branch. Four files changed: `lib.rs`, new `workspace_factory.rs`, `workspace_registry.rs`, and `RUST_AUTHORITY_SNAPSHOT_INTEGRATION_DESIGN.md`.
- **First-pass review:** inspected platform dispatch, selected-root admission, Linux/macOS adapter construction, Windows volume resolution and path conversion, registry ownership transfer, and integration-design limits. The adapters retain native handles and no stdio protocol, writes, patch action or command execution was introduced by this PR.
- **Concrete finding — Windows selection conversion:** `windows_selection_to_volume_root` passes the caller's UTF-16 path to `GetVolumePathNameW` without rejecting embedded NUL code units or explicitly bounding input length, then converts the original path with `to_string_lossy()` and uses that lossy string for security-sensitive mount-prefix and relative-component checks. This is not guaranteed to represent the exact UTF-16 path resolved by Win32, particularly for non-Unicode Windows paths, and embedded NUL can result in truncation at the API boundary. A review comment was posted to PR #131 (comment [#7194545156](https://github.com/Veeresh-11/Loco-Agent/pull/131#issuecomment-7194545156)).
- **Required fix:** reject embedded NUL/overlong input before FFI; do prefix and component processing on exact UTF-16 values returned by Win32 or reject non-Unicode paths explicitly; add Windows tests for NUL, non-Unicode UTF-16, extended-length paths, root-volume and mount-prefix boundaries; keep UNC/network paths fail-closed. The Windows adapter is x86_64-only; the support matrix must say so. macOS firmlink and Windows `OBJ_DONT_REPARSE` contract questions remain separate native acceptance blockers.
- **Exact-head CI:** Rust Core Tests run [#45437420180](https://github.com/Veeresh-11/Loco-Agent/actions/runs/45437420180) passed all nine configured jobs (Ubuntu 22.04/24.04/26.04, Windows Server 2022/2025, macOS 15 arm64/Intel, macOS 26 arm64/Intel). Contract Validation [#45437420337](https://github.com/Veeresh-11/Loco-Agent/actions/runs/45437420337) passed. No status checks were returned by the combined-status endpoint, so the workflow runs are the evidence of record.
- **Review state:** PR #131 had no submitted reviews or inline review threads at the time of inspection; no independent approval is claimed. The review note was added to `docs/security/RUST_AUTHORITY_SNAPSHOT_INTEGRATION_DESIGN.md` on the PR #131 branch in commit `138e5f023a62b991164dee63d22a5e0889604144`.
- **Review register:** PR #131 has now received a first-pass review with a concrete finding. The prior 4/20 register is not incremented to 5/20 until the tracker denominator/review scope is reconciled; record this as a separate review item in the current count. No PR merge, branch rewrite, or privileged feature activation was performed.
- **Result / next task:** PR #131 is CI-green but not accepted pending the Windows UTF-16 path-conversion remediation and independent review. After the correction and exact-head CI, continue the ordered source review with the next unreviewed protocol/authority integration unit (#132–#139), while preserving the Order 2 native API blockers and governance prerequisites.


## Execution update — 2026-11-26: PR #131 Windows selected-path validation remediation

- Re-read this canonical tracker and resumed the next outstanding action from the #131 review: remediate the Windows selected-root conversion finding before advancing to another code-review unit.
- Updated `core/loco-core/src/workspace_factory.rs` on `security/native-workspace-adapter-factory-v1`. The Windows factory no longer uses `to_string_lossy()` for security-sensitive path decisions. - Finding 2 — message-type/version error conflation: all four request validators return InvalidProtocolVersion when message_type is wrong. Version mismatch and malformed/unknown message type are distinct protocol failures; introduce a distinct message-type validation error and tests for hello, registration, capture, and revocation. Otherwise a future handler may map a malformed message to the wrong external error class.

## Execution update — 2026-11-26: PR #135 protocol v2 session/handler first-pass review

- Re-read this canonical tracker and checked live PR #131, #133, and #135 metadata. #131 remains open/draft with independent review outstanding. #133 remains open/draft at the reviewed DTO head; its two requested fixes and base reconciliation remain outstanding. PR #135 is open/draft, based on #133 head 805c4fb3cbacfd8f79da87faa06c06342ff3a269, and its reviewed head is 77d0c78b0e6a07685481ff47cba9a58c65ca427a.
- Inspected the complete 10-file PR #135 patch: protocol DTO changes, new session state module, v2 stdio handler, v1/v2 dispatch, registry wire-handle methods, sidecar integration tests, protocol proposal update, and error fixtures. Also inspected the current v2 proposal error/lifecycle contract and the existing v1 error-handling conventions.
- Positive findings: protocol v2 is dispatched separately from v1; DTOs use strict unknown-field rejection; the session enforces one hello, process-lifetime unique IDs, 4,096 accepted requests and terminal shutdown; handlers use the Rust process-local WorkspaceRegistry and native factory; capture metadata/bytes are dropped before acknowledgement; registry drop occurs on shutdown and on EOF by scope; frame reads are bounded at 12 MiB.
- Finding 1 — typed error contract not implemented consistently: the proposal requires typed error envelopes for malformed messages, duplicate IDs, invalid state transitions and unsupported versions. The v2 handler instead propagates most such failures as Err, terminating the process without a protocol error frame. In particular duplicate-ID and session_error mappings are converted to diagnostic strings only. Emit stable error envelopes (without path/OS detail) and add stdio tests for the corresponding cases.
- Finding 2 — registration error classes are collapsed: all register_selected_workspace errors map to INVALID_WORKSPACE_SELECTION. This hides WORKSPACE_CAPACITY_EXCEEDED and conflates invalid roots with entropy/internal failures. Map known registry/factory error variants to the documented stable codes; preserve generic non-disclosing output for unknown failures.
- Finding 3 — lifecycle coverage gaps at the wire boundary: integration tests cover registration/capture/revoke/shutdown, pre-handshake rejection and unknown handle, but do not exercise duplicate IDs, a second hello, post-shutdown requests, request-budget exhaustion, malformed/unknown messages, or EOF invalidation. Add protocol-level tests; session unit tests alone do not validate wire error frames and registry teardown.

## Execution update — 2026-11-27: PR #107 parser review, UTF-8 bound correction, and exhaustive branch queue

### Next-task review and implementation
- Re-read this tracker before selecting the next task. The previous #104 residual-parser task had already been implemented in stacked PR #107; duplicating it in #104 would have been incorrect. The next task was therefore a first-pass code review of PR #107.
- PR #107: [test(macos): harden bulk record parser on current preflight base](https://github.com/Veeresh-11/Loco-Agent/pull/107), branch `test/macos-bulk-record-parser-hardening-v2`, reviewed initial head `677e9bf2859102df37141d3aca4e734ef51a62d1`.
- Reviewed the full one-file patch. Positive findings: parser is extracted into a pure helper; validates exact record length and maximum record size; checks required returned attribute masks; handles `ATTR_CMN_ERROR` only when returned; rejects nonzero per-entry errors; checks 4-byte attrreference alignment, checked offsets, NUL termination, absolute path/no dot components, and name/path overlap; returns raw `ATTR_CMN_FLAGS` as diagnostic data without mapping `SF_NOUNLINK` to `SF_FIRMLINK`. Synthetic tests cover successful records with/without optional error field, malformed/truncated/oversized records, missing attributes, nonzero errors, unterminated strings, relative paths, negative/misaligned/overlapping references and fixed-field overlap.
- **Finding fixed on branch:** the name attribute was still bounded to 256 bytes. Apple's XNU `getattrlist(2)` documentation states UTF-8 `ATTR_CMN_NAME` data may be `NAME_MAX * 3 + 1` bytes (766 bytes on Darwin), so the old limit rejected valid maximum-length UTF-8 names. See [XNU getattrlist(2), ATTR_CMN_NAME](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/main/bsd/man/man2/getattrlist.2#L2939-L2958). Changed the bound to `3 * 255 + 1` and added exact-maximum and oversized regression fixtures.
- Commits pushed to #107 branch: `242ca636a78ad51dbd3cc86db59c10299dd04dd5` (bound and tests), then `0af5c5f8eb231254f0d82ea2b01c00f75d497a90` (fix fixture NUL terminator). PR conversation comment [#7218001141](https://github.com/Veeresh-11/Loco-Agent/pull/107#issuecomment-7218001141) records the finding, fix and outstanding gates. GitHub rejected a formal REQUEST_CHANGES review because the PR is authored by the repository owner; the finding was therefore recorded as a PR conversation comment, not a submitted independent review.
- Exact-head CI for `0af5c5f...` has **not** appeared in the refreshed workflow/status endpoints. Earlier green Rust Core Tests #45346723420 and Contract Validation #45346723355 apply to prior head `677e9bf...` only; do not transfer those results to the new head.
- PR #107 is open/draft and branch ancestry is stale: comparison against declared base `test/macos-firmlink-extended-bulk-evidence` is 15 commits ahead / 4 behind (merge base `8c4a2d0...`; current base head `cc7758783760ca5c940fe5d438cae445322a585d`). Reconcile the branch with the current base and rerun exact-head Rust Core Tests plus Contract Validation before marking the task complete or considering integration.
- The native `SF_FIRMLINK` discrepancy remains a separate acceptance blocker. Parser correctness is not proof of firmlink authorization; issue #74 and independent native security review remain open.
